~-FOREWORD
The Purattirattu is an anthology of 1,570 stanzas. It may be assigned to
the latter half of the 15th century, and it must have been put together at a
period when many Buddhist and Jaina works, now lost, were available.
Kamban's Ramavataram is the latest work represented in the collection, if
we leave aside the one stanza ascribed to Nalavenba in some of the Mss. and
to Bharatam in others.
The anthology is of considerable importance to students of Tamil literature. It brings to light many works which are otherwise unknown, e.g.,
Asiriyamalai,

Irumpal-kanci,

Narada-caritai,

Santi-puranam

and

Perumporul-vilakkam. It gives many excerpts from works known only by
name or by a few extracts preserved by later scholiasts; among such works
are Kundalakesi, Takaduryattirai, Muttollayiram and Valaiyapati. It in-

cludes extracts from the missing portions of works now accessible in more
ot less extensive fragments, e.g., Patirrupattu, Paripadal, Bharatam and

Cirupancamulam. It cites from certain works, a few stanzas which, however,
are not found in their published editions. Lastly, it offers better texts of
many poems already printed from inferior manuscripts.

From another standpoint also, the collection is important. It follows,
in its subject analysis, the scheme of the Tirukkural and forms and excellent
of a high order, and
supplement to that great work. The poems excerpted are
idiom from which
forceful
and
terse
simple,

of
constitute fine examples

puṟattiraṭṭu

xii

After the text had been printed, I gained access, through the kindness

of Mahamahopadhyaya Dr. V. Swaminatha Aiyar, to a valuable paper
manuscript in his possession. The variant readings given by this manuscript
are noted in Appendix V. Again, one of the cadjan manuscript belongs to
the Tanjore Palace Library. The rules of that library are strict and do not
permit the loan of any manuscript, and at my request Mr. S. Subrahmanya
Sastrigal, Retired Sanskrit Pandit of the Kalyanasundaram High

School,

Tanjore, kindly noted down the readings on the proofs which I sent to him.
Another cadjan manuscript belonging to the Tamil Sangam Library was
compared by me at Madura with the assistance of Mr. T.K. Ramanuja
Aiyangar, Assistant Editor, Sen Tamil.

In noting readings, I have adopted the practice which I have been following for some years now. In this system, the text is not encumbered with
any superior numbers. In the footnote, the readings of each stanza are noted
against the number of the cir where the variation occurs.

system to the suggestion of my friend Mr. P.N.

I owe this neat

Appuswami

Aiyar,

B.A.,B.L., Advocate, Madras, By its simplicity, it enables one to present

the manuscript material in its entirety, including scribal errors. It must be
noted that even these errors might ultimately prove a valuable guide in
arriving at a correct reading.

Beyond stanza 1305, the paper manuscript of the Government
Oriental Manuscripts Library was the only source and the text was
obviously corrupt at many points. I owe a new

suggestions of possible

readings to the kindness of Dr. V. Swaminatha Aiyar and Mahavidvan R.
Raghava Aiyangar

of the Annamalai University.

Their suggestions

are

included in Appendix V.

In comparing manuscripts and in proof-reading, I was assisted by Mr.
M.

Arunachalam

Pillai, B.A., of the Government Translator's Office,
Madras, Vidvan V. Venkatarajulu Reddiyar, Junior Lecturer in Tamil, O.R.
Institute and Vidvan A. Narayanaswami, Research Student in Tamil, O.R.

Institute, were also of occasibnal help. Mr. T.K. Rajamarthandam Pillai,
B.A., Tamil Lexicon Office, made for me a copy of the manuscript
belonging to the Government Oriental Manuscripts Library.

1am happy to acknowledge my indebtedness to the above scholars
and

authorities who lent me manuscripts of this work and helped me in other
ways.

puṟattiraṭṭu

xili

The matter contained in the Tamil Introduction appeared ina series of
articles on “Tokai-nul" in Kalaimakal. By the courtesy of its editor, Mr. R.
Narayanaswami Aiyar, B.A., B.L., I have incorporated the articles here
with such changes as were suggested by further study.
The Proprietor of the G.S. Press has devoted considerable care to the
neat and accurate printing of the book and my thanks are due to him.

University of Madras,

S. Vaiyapuri Pillai,

10th March, 1938.

Reader in Tamil.

NOTE ON THE REPRINT
The First edition of this classical anthology having been exhausted, a
reprint had to be issued urgently to meet the public demand. In this reprint,
the Tamil Indroduction has been slightly modified in a few places. A stanza

from Asiriyamalai (St.1332) has been split into two separate stanzas as
originally suggested by Dr. V. Swaminatha Aiyar and the new stanza bears
the number 1332. No other material alteration is introduced.
University of Madras,

20th ṣī, 1939.

8. Vaiyapuri Pillai,

Reader in Tamil.

mutalāvatāka

puṟattiraṭṭu

xxii

iṅṅaṉam mutalāvatāka vaittuḷḷatu mēṟkaṇakku nālkaḷākiya
tokai nūlkaḷukkum ēṉaiya &ḻkkaṇakku nūlkaḷukkum iṭai
nikartta taṉmaiyuṭaiyatāy innūl amainta kāraṇattatāṟ pōlum.
nām iṅkē mukkiyamākak kavaṉikkavuḷḷatu eḷitil varaiyaṟai
ceyyamuṭiyāta puṟamum akamumākiya poruṭ kūṟupāṭṭiṉai mēṟ
koḷḷātu nītiyaip poruḷākakkoṇṭu aṟam poruḷ iṉpam
eṉṟa pākupāṭṭiṉaip piṉpaṟṟi nālaṭiyār amaintatu eṉpatākum.
eṉavē ittokainūl nītiyākiya piṟitoru neṟipaṟṟit tokukkap
paṭṭatu.

camayam paṟṟiyaṉa
itaṉaiyaṭuttu namatu maṉakkaṇmuṉ varum tokainūl camayac
cārpākavuḷḷatu. caivat tirumuṟaikaḷum vaiṣṇavaratu tālāyira
tivyap pirapantamum uṇmaiyil tokairanūlkaḷē yeṉṟu karutat
takkaṉa. cēkkiḻār aruḷic ceyta tiruttoṇṭar purāṇattait
tavirttu ēṉaiya 17 tirumuṟaikaḷum tokuttavar nampiyāṇṭār
nampikaḷ. iṅṅaṉamē nālāyira tivyap pirapattattait tokuttavar
srīman nātamuṉikaḷ. ivaṟṟait tokai nūlkaḷākak koḷḷutaliṟ
karuttoṟṟumai yillātu pōyiṉum, oṉpatām tirumuṟaiyaiyum,
patiṉorām tirumuṟaiyaiyum, iyaṟpāvaiyum tokainūl eṉak
koḷaṉḷutalil karuttu vēṟupāṭu nikaḻtaṟku iṭamiṉṟu. oṉpatām

veṉa vuṇarttappaṭukiṉṟaṉa. itaṉāl ikkālattu iṟantupaṭṭa pala
naralkaḷiṉiṉṟum palaceyyuṭkaḷ kiṭaikkiṉṟaṉa. icceyti tamiḻ

pālatu. ōr atikārattil oru nūliliruntu oru ceyyuḷ tarappaṭa
ṭirukkumāyiṉ, acceyyuḷukkuppiṉ annūṟpeyar kāṇappaṭum

nūṟpeyar kuṟikkappaṭum. nūṟpeyarukkup piṉvaruvatu vēṟu nālaic
cārnta ceyyuḷākavirukkum. immuṟaiyaippiṉpaṟṟiyē puṟattiraṭṭu
muḻuvatilum mūlanūlkaḷ kāṭṭappaṭṭuḷḷaṉa. oru cilaviṭaṅkaḷil
nūṟpeyarkaḷ tavaṟākavuḷḷaṉa veṉpatu uṇmai, orucilliṭaṅ
kaḷil nūṟpeyarkaḷ vevvēṟāka māṟupaṭṭum piratikaḷil uḷḷaṉa.

eṉpatu ippeyarai vaḻaṅkat toṭaṅkiyavar karuttākal vēṇṭum.

muḻuvatum akappaṭṭuḷḷa nūlkaḷ
iṉi, muḻuvatum akappaṭṭu veḷiyiṭappeṟṟuḷḷa nūlkaḷuḷ

oru cilavaṟṟiṟkum puṟattiraṭṭu utavukiṉṟatu. nūlkaḷ ciṟantaṉavāyiṉ,
palarum kaṟṟup pōṟṟutaṟkuriya takutiyum poruḷ viḷakkamu muṭai
yaṉavāyiṉ, antūṟpiratikaḷ tamiḻ nāṭṭil mikap palavāyk kiṭaip
paṉavām. piratikaḷ mikuntōṟum pāṭapētaṅkaḷum mikaicceyyuṭ
kaḷum perukak kāṇappaṭum. nāḷaṭaivil uṇmaiyāṉa pāṭaṅkaḷai
varaiyaṟuppatum nūṟkuriya ceyyuṭṭokai iṉaitteṉat tuṇi
vatum eḷitil iyalātavāy muṭiyum. piḻaikaḷum mikutiyākap palku
taṟku iṭamuṇṭu. ceyyuṭṭokai varaiyaṟai, pāṭa niccayam, piḻai
tiruttamākiya immūṉṟaṟkum puṟattiraṭṭup peritum payaṉpaṭukiṉ
ṟatu ceyyuṭṭokaiyai niccayittaliṉkaṇ mikaiyākavuḷḷa ceyyuṭ
kaḷai nōkkutal iṉṟimaiyātatākum.

ceṅkaṇ civanta kaḷattu.

(puṟat. 1427)

itu kiṭaitta kaḷavaḻip piratikaḷiṟ kāṇappeṟavillai. kālañceṉṟa
srī i.vai. aḷantarāmaiyaravarkaḷ patippil 41 ceyyuṭkaḷ uḷḷaṉa.

yuṭaṉ kuṟikkappaṭṭirukkiṉṟatu kaḷavaḻi nāṟpatu eṉat tokai

vēnta rellān taṉṉōk kiṉarē
nōkki nōkkā muṟuvalaṉ ṟākkit
taḻīintā meṉṉat taṇṇumai
kaḻittā ṉoḷvāḷ vīḻntaṉa kaḷiṟē.
īṉṟu puṟantaruta leṉṟalaik kaṭaṉē
cāṉṟō ṉākkutal tantaikkuk kaṭaṉē
vēlvaṭittuk koṭuttal kollaṟkuk kaṭaṉē
taṇṇaṭai nalkal vēntaṟkuk kaṭaṉē
oḷiṟuvāḷ veñcama murukkik
kaḷiṟeṟintu peyartal kāḷaikkuk kaṭaṉē.
